= SPEC_LOG — Discrepancy, Fit and Correction Log
:sectnums:
:sectnumlevels: 5
:toc:
:toclevels: 4

This is the working record behind `SPEC.adoc`. It holds everything `SPEC.adoc`
deliberately does not: provenance, measurement method, superseded readings,
fitted constants, and the discrepancies between what the driver's own code
implies and what the reference renders actually do.

Entries are sorted by date, latest first. Every correction to `SPEC.adoc` is
recorded here together with the evidence that forced it; `SPEC.adoc` itself
carries only the corrected result.

Be wary that an apparent discrepancy is sometimes not a defect in `SPEC.adoc` at
all, but a mechanism `SPEC.adoc` documents and this project has not implemented.
Check before treating a "the spec is silent" claim in this document as fact.

Two numbering series run through the entries and are referred to by number
throughout: **items** (`item1`…`item33`) are discrepancies and gaps between
`SPEC.adoc` and this project's engine; **entries** (`entry1`…`entry16`) are
fitted values, shipped or rejected. The numbers are stable identifiers, not
positions in this document — the entries are ordered by date, not by number.

== Conventions

=== What `[F:fitted]` means

A value tagged `[F:fitted]` in this project's source or docs was tuned
**empirically against reference audio renders** (probe MIDI files vs. their
captured reference audio in `probe-results/`), not recovered from the
original binary's disassembly. It exists because the real consumption code
for that value is `[O]` (outside every disassembled/examined range in
`SPEC.adoc`) -- empirical fitting against reference audio is the sanctioned
fallback for exactly that situation, but it must always be visibly flagged
as a fit, never presented or graded as `[A:recovered]`.

Rules for anything tagged `[F:fitted]`:

* It must cite the probe/reference it was fit against and the exact error
  metric used.
* It must cite where a future RE pass should look to recover the real
  formula (a `SPEC.adoc` pointer, an address, a function), so the fit can be
  retired once real recovery happens.
* It must never be cited as evidence of what the original binary does --
  only as an evidence-based placeholder standing in for it.
* If a fit is measured and found to make things *worse* against the
  project's own regression harness, it is not shipped, but the attempt and
  its measurements are still recorded here (see entry 1) -- an honestly
  reported negative result is not a discarded one.

'''

=== The verification ceiling

Overstating what the reference captures can prove is the single worst failure
mode available to this project. **No stage of it may report bit-exactness as
verified** — not `SPEC.adoc`, not this log, not any test-harness output.

**Capture provenance.** The references were produced by playing MIDI back on
an emulated Windows XP guest and capturing the resulting audio through a
host-side "Stereo Mix"-style loopback device, not by extracting samples from
the driver's own output buffer. That chain is itself a source of noise and
timing artifacts distinct from anything `swmidi.sys` does, and everything
below follows from it.

**Noise floor.** True-silence padding across the probe recordings is confined
to `{-2..2}` LSB, ≈99.98% within `{-1,0,1}`, with near-zero left/right
cross-channel correlation (≈0.001) — a small, near-independent, roughly
±1 LSB dither floor per channel, not signal `[M]`. Lag-1 autocorrelation on
the same quiet regions measures 0.04–0.31 per file (pooled ≈0.135) `[M]`; the
qualitative characterization holds, but no tighter numeric autocorrelation
bound should be repeated as a verified ceiling. **Because of this floor,
bit-exactness cannot be empirically proven with these captures.**
Sample-exactness remains the design goal.

**Repeatability differs by material type.** Reference renders of the same
event repeat to approximately **1% on percussive attacks** and **0.02% on
sustained tones** `[M]`. A single tolerance applied to both will either pass
bad percussive output or fail good sustained output.

**Capture-chain timing offset — negative, file-dependent, not a property of
the synth.** Measured onset-alignment offsets between a probe manifest's
declared MIDI event time and the corresponding audible onset are **−0.688 s**
(probe 20: declared 1.200 s, measured 0.512 s) and **−0.802 s** (probe 18)
`[M]`. The magnitude is stable within a single probe file's own event train
but differs materially between files, which is itself the evidence that this
is a per-capture-session artifact — differing lead-in silence, or a differing
capture-clock zero point — not a property of `swmidi.sys`. **Always recover
alignment by cross-correlation against the actual audio; never assume a fixed
offset**, and never use either figure to calibrate timing inside the engine.

**The 22050→44100 upsampling stage is a hard ceiling on any 44.1 kHz
comparison.** Zero-order hold and linear interpolation are both eliminated
(0.23% and 0.87% of odd-indexed samples match) `[M]`, and `SPEC.adoc`
xref:SPEC.adoc#s6-11[§6.11] recovers the real stage's algorithm class from
`kmixer.sys` itself — but its tap coefficients remain `[O]`. The only
ceiling-free comparison is at 22050 Hz.

**Per-reference provenance differs, and this bounds how much of each one's
residual is attributable to the engine rather than to how the file was
captured.**

* `field/corridor.flac` was captured over the same loopback path but **with a
  DAW application left open** on the capturing machine. The user who produced
  it reports it sounds measurably brighter than the other two. A DAW held
  open plausibly forced the guest or host device to a different operating
  rate (e.g. 48000 Hz), moving the upsampling stage away from the clean 2×
  case and/or adding host-side processing to the loopback path. This was not
  confirmed spectrally — the three references are three different pieces of
  music, so content differences dominate any spectral-tilt comparison, and no
  matched-content A/B exists. **Recorded as user-reported capture provenance,
  not as a measured value.** Net effect: an unknown, plausibly
  larger-than-dither share of this file's residual is capture-path artifact,
  so its exact spectral match is not a precision target.
* `field/Kot_and_A64-GENERAL_SERUM.flac` was captured over the same path with
  **no DAW open**, deliberately un-normalized so it clips naturally at the
  int16 rail — 1.06% of samples sit at `+32767`/`-32768` `[M]` (a separately
  reported 1.07% for the same file differs only by measurement-tool
  rounding). The cleanest of the three; treated as the acceptance reference.
* `field/Strobe-faffaeefafaefae.flac` was also captured with no DAW open.
  Also clean; treated as the aliasing-model acid test.

All three sit above the ±1 LSB dither floor. GENERAL SERUM and faffaee are
weighted **above** corridor as fidelity targets.

None of this weakens the findings already recorded about the engine's own
render behaviour against `field/corridor.mid` — the release-time bug that
left it truncated (~70 s to 82.8 s across snapshots, never latching finished,
against a ~52.4 s reference) and its fix (52.57 s against a 52.43 s
reference, xref:#item15[item 15]), together with the onset-timing accuracy
check on the same file, concern only the engine's internal timing and
termination. They are independent of which capture path recorded the
reference, and they stand.

'''

=== Harness re-baseline, 2026-07-25

The project's Python fitting/comparison harness (a corpus-comparison driver
plus per-feature fit-check scripts) is not retained in this tree; every
mention of it below (and of its individual scripts and recorded JSON
results by name) is historical narration of a check that was run at the
time, not a pointer to a file that exists now. Its rendering role is now
filled by the native `dist/msgs-render` binary. This harness's alignment was replaced (see
`envelope_align_offset` and the two-stage alignment in `align_and_grade`). Every
`compare_spectral_22050.overall_db` figure in this file that was recorded
before this change was computed on a **misaligned** reference/render pair
for a large part of the corpus, and is therefore not comparable with any
figure recorded after it.

**What was wrong.** The old aligner cross-correlated raw waveforms over the
whole file. The probe corpus is mostly ladders of *identical repeated notes*
at fixed spacing, so the waveform correlates nearly as well one whole note-
slot off as at the true offset, and the FFT peak routinely locked onto the
wrong slot. Confirmed on the gain/pan probes, where every disagreement with
a hand-measured offset was an exact integer multiple of that probe's own
note spacing: 03_velocity off by 8.07 s, 24_gain_staging by 8.11 s,
25_pan_law by exactly two notes.

**The fix.** Two stages: a coarse pass correlating 10 ms **RMS envelopes**
(the envelope keeps the per-step *level* that distinguishes one rung of a
ladder from the next, which is exactly what waveform correlation discards),
then a sample-domain refine within ±50 ms of the coarse result, so the final
offset is still sample-accurate. Validated against seven independently
hand-measured offsets: max error **29 ms**, five of seven within 6 ms, versus
errors up to 8.11 s before. All seven `IMPLAUSIBLE_OFFSET` flags in the
corpus cleared (7 → 0); envelope correlation r = 0.86–0.98.

**Scale of the correction.** 14 of 24 graded probes were misaligned. Corpus
mean −8.025 → **−10.113 dB**; GENERAL_SERUM −2.27 → **−4.24 dB** with its
envelope correlation r 0.642 → **0.831**.

**These are measurement corrections, not synth improvements.** Not one line
of `src/engine/` changed between those two numbers. The synth was always this
close; the harness was mis-reporting it.

**What this does and does not invalidate.** Conclusions drawn from *direct
per-step measurement at a hand-recovered alignment* stand unaffected —
notably xref:entry7[Entry 7]'s per-channel pan table and the `[M: probe 07]` settlements
in `SPEC_LOG` §4 and §9, all of which used RMS-envelope alignment
precisely because the harness number was visibly untrustworthy. What is
invalidated is any *corpus-wide comparison table* computed by the old
harness, including xref:entry7[Entry 7] §4 (re-measured — see the note there) and the
before/after tables in Entries 1–6. Those tables are kept as recorded; they
are history, not current measurements.

'''

'''

[#item25-followup-gm-off-measured]
== 2026-07-29 — GM System Off's silence is SETTLED by measurement (`[M: probes 47/48]`); the collection release is now SHIPPED

Follow-up to xref:#item25-resolved[the `0x150bc` acquire/release entry], and
specifically its "Not implemented: the collection release" section. That
entry read the teardown from the driver's own code (`[A]`) but left its
audible consequence `[I]`, pending "an `[M]` probe" against
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-9-1[S4.9.1] and xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]. Both probes it
proposed — `probes/47_gm_off_state.mid` and `probes/48_rhythm_part_reset.mid`
— now have reference renders (`probe-results/47.flac`, `probe-results/48.flac`,
9.8148 s and 6.6467 s, 44100 Hz/16-bit stereo), and this entry reads them.

=== Method

Both files were decoded with `ffmpeg`. Each file's own capture-clock offset
was derived independently, per case, from the first non-digital-zero sample
after that case's nominal MIDI note-on, and used only to align that file's
own windows — never assumed, never carried between files. Probe 47 runs
0.458 s ahead of nominal (0.4574 s at case 1, 0.4581 s at case 3, 0.4588 s at
case 4 — 1.4 ms spread across the file); probe 48 runs 0.6807 s ahead, with
both of its note-ons landing within 0.7 ms of nominal. Level windows are an
0.85 s body starting 50 ms after onset (probe 47) or the plain
nominal-equivalent spans given below (probe 48). The harmonic table used a
200 ms Hann-windowed FFT starting 300 ms after each onset, read at the
fundamental and its 2nd/3rd/4th/5th/7th partials. Probe 47 case 2's silence
was confirmed by `ffmpeg astats` over the file window 1.15–5.135 s reporting
Min level 0.000000 / Max level 0.000000 / Peak level −inf / RMS level −inf
across 175,739 samples — bracketing case 2's whole aligned note with over
1.4 s of margin on each side — which is bit-exactness confirmed by `astats`,
not an RMS threshold.

=== Probe 47 — per-case sounding span and level

[options="header"]
|===
| Case | Nominal note | Sounds (file time) | Peak dBFS | RMS dBFS
| 1 (control, after GS Reset) | 0.6–1.6 | 0.1426–1.1485 | −10.30 | −13.386
| 2 (after GM System Off) | 3.1–4.1 | nothing | −inf | −inf
| 3 (after GM System On) | 5.6–6.6 | 5.1419–6.1609 | −11.26 | −23.839
| 4 (control, after GS Reset) | 8.1–9.1 | 7.6412–8.6474 | −10.30 | −13.387
|===

=== Probe 47 — harmonic content (dB relative to the 261.63 Hz fundamental)

[options="header"]
|===
| Case | 2nd | 3rd | 4th | 5th | 7th | Reads as
| 1 | −45.0 | −51.8 | −58.8 | −61.5 | −67.2 | Sine Wave
| 2 | — | — | — | — | — | silent (fundamental bin at −667 dBFS, numerical zero)
| 3 | −12.3 | −2.9 | −14.4 | −4.5 | −8.7 | Square Wave
| 4 | −45.1 | −51.8 | −58.8 | −61.5 | −67.2 | Sine Wave
|===

Cases 1 and 4 agree to 0.1 dB on every harmonic and to 0.001 dB on body RMS.
Both patches sustain — 100 ms slices hold case 1 flat at −10.30 dBFS peak and
case 3 flat at −14.4 dBFS peak for the whole 1 s note — so case 2's silence
is not a fast decay. Release tails after note-off: 7 ms (case 1, sine), 20 ms
(case 3, square).

=== Probe 48 — levels, ratio and pan

[options="header"]
|===
| Window (nominal) | L RMS dBFS | R RMS dBFS
| first pair 1.05–2.45 | −49.75 | −49.08
| gap 2.60–4.45 | −inf (exact zero) | −inf (exact zero)
| retrigger 4.55–5.95 | −20.50 | −20.40
|===

Retrigger/first-pair linear amplitude ratio: L 29.02× (+29.25 dB), R 27.16×
(+28.68 dB), stereo power 28.03× (+28.95 dB) — against the squared-gain-law
prediction (100·127)²/(40·60)² = 28.00× for volume `40`→`100` and expression
`60`→`127`.

[options="header"]
|===
| Pair | 261.63 Hz (ch1, sent hard left) | 329.63 Hz (ch2, sent hard right)
| first | +25.76 dB | −25.55 dB
| retrigger | −0.10 dB | −0.10 dB
|===

Cut shape: a 2 ms peak envelope holds steady between −40.5 and −42.3 dBFS
right through the window starting at nominal 2.4615, reads −90.3 dBFS at
2.4635, and is exact digital zero from 2.4655 onward — full level to digital
zero in ≤2 ms, the resolution of this measurement. As a measurement caveat,
not a driver finding: the cut lands 36.5 ms before the SysEx's nominal
2.500 s, in a file whose two note-ons are accurate to 0.7 ms; this probe
cannot tell whether the driver applies the reset out-of-band or the capture
chain delivered the SysEx early.

=== Engine vs. reference

`dist/msgs-render` in the working tree is stale (mtime Jul 28; `src/` last
changed Jul 29) and predates the USE RHYTHM PART change
(xref:#item25-resolved[the prior entry]). The figures below are from a binary
built fresh from `src/` at HEAD (`gcc -std=c11 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Isrc`) into
a scratch directory; neither `dist/` nor `src/` were modified to take them.

[options="header"]
|===
| Metric | Reference | Engine (fresh build)
| Probe 47 case 1 body RMS | −13.386 dBFS | −16.37 dBFS
| Probe 47 case 2 body RMS | −inf (silent) | −26.85 dBFS (sounds, Square Wave)
| Probe 47 case 3 body RMS | −23.839 dBFS | −26.85 dBFS
| Probe 47 case 4 body RMS | −13.387 dBFS | −16.37 dBFS
| Probe 48 first pair L / R RMS | −49.75 / −49.08 dBFS | −52.73 / −52.06 dBFS
| Probe 48 retrigger L / R RMS | −20.50 / −20.40 dBFS | −23.46 / −23.39 dBFS
| Probe 48 retrigger ratio, stereo power | 28.03× (+28.95 dB) | L 29.08× / R 27.14× (+29.27 / +28.67 dB)
| Probe 48 cut duration to digital zero | ≤2 ms | ~46 ms, with a ~+9 dB rise en route
|===

The engine's probe 47 case 2 sounds a Square Wave at a level identical to
case 3's — exactly what not implementing the collection release predicts,
since GM System Off already clears GS mode in the engine
(xref:#item25-resolved[the prior entry]) but does not release the collection.
`score.py` against that fresh build: `47_gm_off_state` r 0.961 / −18.51 dB /
dead 30 ms; `48_rhythm_part_reset` r 0.982 / −35.90 dB / dead 0 ms. The
checked-in stale binary reads `48_rhythm_part_reset` at r 0.969 / −30.99 dB
instead — a reminder to rebuild before scoring this item.

Worth recording as a measurement caveat, not a claimed engine defect: the
engine's renders sit a uniform 2.97–2.98 dB below both of these new
reference captures, on every channel of both files (47 case 1 −16.37 vs
−13.39; 48 first pair L −52.73 vs −49.75; 48 retrigger L −23.46 vs −20.50).
This offset is unexplained and invisible to `score.py`, which normalizes by
mean RMS before the residual — recorded here as a capture-ceiling
observation, not investigated further.

=== What this settles, and what it does not

**Settles.** GM System Off releases the instrument collection, and the
audible consequence is total silence — xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-9-1[S4.9.1]'s
closing sentence moves from `[I]` to `[M]`. The positive controls hold:
cases 1 and 4 are Sine Wave, identical to each other, so case 2's silence
cannot be blamed on the probe file. Case 3 being Square Wave rather than
Sine Wave confirms, audibly, that the re-acquiring GM System On both cleared
GS mode (bank 8 no longer selectable) and genuinely re-acquired (it sounds at
all). Probe 48 confirms the USE RHYTHM PART tail quantitatively against the
reference: sounding voices cut at the message, and the retrigger returns
28.03× louder in stereo power (28.00× predicted) and centred to within
0.1 dB, with no controller message between the two note-ons.

**Does NOT settle.** Whether a voice already sounding when GM System Off
arrives holds a reference of its own and so survives the release — the
refcount question at `[A:0x16066]` — stays `[O]`. Nothing was sounding when
probe 47's System Off arrived (case 1's tail ends at file 1.1485, the message
lands at file 2.042), so this probe cannot speak to it. Traced from
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5] and xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-2[S4.6.2]: on the GM path
the driver calls `ResetDevice`, then the collection release at `0x13700`,
then falls into the shared reset tail, and that tail's
`ResetAllChannelControllers` reclaims every sounding voice immediately. The
voice is therefore cut under *both* hypotheses, so a probe with the System Off
landed while a note is still sounding bounds the question rather than settling
it — the only window in which "the voice holds a reference" and "it does not"
differ audibly lies between the release call and the tail, shorter than one
render buffer. What such a probe CAN establish is the first `[M]` on what
actually happens to a voice sounding across GM System Off — whether it is
cut, how fast, and whether any noise burst or truncation appears at the
boundary — not the refcount question itself. The `[O]` stands.

Also does not settle, and cannot be built as a probe file at all: re-acquiring
via MIDI System Reset (`0xFF`) instead of GM System On. Inside an SMF track,
`0xFF` is the meta-event prefix — this project's own reader treats it as one
(`src/engine/smf.c:78`) — and the Standard MIDI File format has no encoding
for a realtime System Reset. Verified by hex-dumping
`artifacts/probes/47_gm_off_state.mid`: its only `FF` bytes are the `FF 51`
tempo meta and `FF 2F` end-of-track. So the System Reset re-acquire path
stays `[A:0x13218]` and would need a live MIDI-stream capture, not a probe
MIDI, to reach `[M]`.

[#item32]
=== Item 32 — the reference's USE RHYTHM PART cut is instantaneous; the engine's passed through a bump. SHIPPED

Probe 48 exposed a discrepancy nothing had measured before: the reference's
voice cut on USE RHYTHM PART is effectively instantaneous (≤2 ms to digital
zero, no level change beforehand), while the engine's took about 46 ms and
passed through a ~+9 dB bump on the way. The audible part was that bump: the
engine applied the reset's restored Volume/Expression defaults to voices that
were still releasing, instead of cutting them at once.

**Root cause.** `reset_all_channel_controllers_device()` (`synth.c:200-208`,
this engine's shared tail for the GS Reset / GM System On / GM System Off /
System Reset device-reset paths and USE RHYTHM PART) was calling
`voice_all_sound_off(i)` once per channel. `voice_all_sound_off()` performs
`start_release(v, 0)`, an authored envelope release — correct for its other
callers (CC120/CC126/CC127, where xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-3[S4.3] says those rows
"release (do not cut)") but wrong for the device-reset tail, whose `0x123de`
(xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-2[S4.6.2], xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-3[S5.3]) is an immediate
reclaim to the free pool, not an authored release.

**Fix.** `reset_all_channel_controllers_device()` now calls
`voice_pool_reset()` once instead of `voice_all_sound_off(i)` per channel
(`synth.c:200-208`). `voice_all_sound_off()` itself was not modified and
`voice.c` was not touched, so the CC120/123/126/127 release paths are
unchanged. One deliberate deviation, already commented in the code:
`voice_pool_reset()` also zeroes the reserve top-up accumulator and the voice
age counter, which `0x123de` does not — harmless because every voice has just
gone inactive.

**Direct measurement, before -> after (probe 48 cut, engine render, 2 ms peak
envelope):** before the fix the level rose to −34.7 dBFS at 2.506 s and did
not reach digital zero until 2.546 s. After the fix the level is flat between
−43.7 and −45.1 dBFS right up to the bin at 2.4960 s, and is digital zero
from the bin at 2.4980 s onward — the last nonzero sample is at 2.4965 s: a
fall to digital zero inside one 2 ms bin, with the ~+9 dB bump gone. Against
the reference, whose cut lands at nominal-equivalent 2.4635 s, the engine now
carries 33.0 ms of sound where the reference is already silent, down from
82.5 ms before the fix.

**Refinement at sample resolution.** Both "effectively instantaneous (≤2 ms
to digital zero, no level change beforehand)" above and "to digital zero
inside one 2 ms bin" for the fix were measured at 2 ms hop resolution, and
neither is wrong at that resolution. A later, per-sample look at a different
cut — probe 49's GM-System-Off cut, xref:#item33[Item 33] — shows the
reference's collapse actually spans about 68 us at 44100 Hz (not truly
instantaneous), while our engine's is a single-sample step. This refines the
resolution of the measurement, not the fix: the fix (removing the ~46 ms
fade and the ~+9 dB bump) still stands and is still SHIPPED.

=== What changed

* xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-9-1[S4.9.1]: the closing sentence replaced in full —
  silence-until-a-GM-System-On-re-acquires is now `[M]`; the System Reset
  re-acquire path stays `[A]` on its call site (`[A:0x13218]`), separated out
  of the `[M]`; the sounding-voice refcount question stays `[O]`, still
  citing `[A:0x16066]`.
* xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]: the USE RHYTHM PART row's Evidence cell gains an
  `[M]` alongside its existing `[A]` citations.

=== What shipped

GM System Off now releases the instrument collection. Implemented with no
new state: `dls_find_region()` already returns null when `g_dls.valid` is 0
and `voice_note_on()` already allocates no voice in that case, which is
exactly the driver's "FindInstrument's scan finds nothing" — so the release
is `g_dls.valid = 0`.

* `synth.c:37-43` — new `acquire_collection()`, guarded on
  `g_dls.first_instrument` so a re-acquire cannot resurrect a gm.dls that
  never parsed.
* `synth.c:82-87` — `synth_reset()` acquires at its end. `synth_reset()` is
  this engine's device-construction/open path as well as its System Reset
  (`0xFF`) and GM System On/Off handler, and xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-9-1[S4.9.1]'s
  call-site table has all three of those acquiring.
* `synth.c:297-303` — GM System Off (sub-ID2 `0x02`) sets `g_dls.valid = 0`
  after that acquire.
* `synth.c:318-323` — GS Reset saves and restores `g_dls.valid` around
  `synth_reset()`, because xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5] says GS Reset does not
  call the acquire/release at all.

Item 32's fix (above) shipped in the same pass: `reset_all_channel_controllers_device()`
(`synth.c:200-208`) now calls `voice_pool_reset()` once instead of
`voice_all_sound_off(i)` per channel.

=== Corpus gate, before -> after

Full 66-item corpus, same binary rebuilt before and after: the "before"
binary built from a clean `git worktree` at HEAD (i.e. the pre-change
`src/engine/synth.c`), the "after" binary from the working tree, both with
the same `gcc -std=c11 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Isrc` invocation, both scored
against the same corpus via `MSGS_RENDER=<binary> .venv/bin/python
artifacts/score.py`.

[options="header"]
|===
| Item | Before | After
| `CrystalOscillator` | r 0.847 / −22.76 dB | r 0.847 / −22.74 dB
| `HueArme-Weekend` | r 0.765 / −19.34 dB | r 0.766 / −19.38 dB
| `47_gm_off_state` | r 0.961 / −18.51 dB | r 0.992 / −48.76 dB
| `48_rhythm_part_reset` | r 0.982 / −35.90 dB | r 0.982 / −35.56 dB
| MEAN | r 0.927 / −31.06 dB | r 0.927 / −31.52 dB
|===

A line-by-line diff of the two full score tables — this time a true
full-corpus run, not the truncated capture the earlier version of this entry
was written from — shows this is the complete and exhaustive set of
differences: every other one of the 66 items is byte-identical between the
two tables. `CrystalOscillator` and `HueArme-Weekend` both carry USE RHYTHM
PART, so Item 32's fix is what reaches them. CrystalOscillator's residual
moved 0.02 dB the wrong way and HueArme-Weekend's moved 0.04 dB the right way
with `r` up 0.001 — both changes are far too small to read as evidence either
way, and they are reported here only because the earlier "no other item
moved" claim was wrong. The shipping decision rests on the targeted probe
(47) improving by 30.25 dB and the corpus mean improving by 0.46 dB with `r`
unchanged.

Direct measurement, probe
47 case 2, engine render, body window 3.15–4.05 s: RMS is now exactly 0 and
the largest absolute sample in the whole 0.9 s window is 0 — bit-exact
silence, matching the reference. Cases 1/3/4 are unchanged at −16.37, −26.85,
−16.37 dBFS. (Probe 48's direct cut measurement is recorded under Item 32
above.)

**Honest note on probe 48's residual.** Probe 48's whole-file
level-normalized spectral residual moved 0.34 dB the *wrong* way (−35.90 ->
−35.56) even though the behaviour it targets measurably improved. Its
envelope r is unchanged at 0.982, and the corpus mean improved by 0.46 dB.
The residual is a whole-file average over a 6.6 s render dominated by the
two 1.4 s note groups and cannot resolve a ~50 ms cut window, so the cut was
accepted on the direct 2 ms-resolution measurement above rather than on this
residual. The 0.34 dB itself is not explained here; no mechanism for it is
claimed.

**Corpus SysEx census — supersedes xref:#item25-resolved[the prior entry]'s
"no corpus item carries either message".** That sentence came from an
unverified assertion, not a parse. A proper SysEx parse of every corpus MIDI
(parsing MTrk events and reading actual F0 SysEx payloads, not scanning raw
bytes) finds ten corpus files carrying GM System On and/or USE RHYTHM PART:

[options="header"]
|===
| File | Messages carried
| `field/CrystalOscillator.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `field/HueArme-Weekend.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `field/Kot_and_A64-GENERAL_SERUM.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `field/eek_the.mid` | GM System On
| `field/serum_opening.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `tests/lazers.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `tests/radio.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `tests/sine-gate.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `tests/sine2.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
| `tests/wild-sweep.mid` | GM System On, USE RHYTHM PART
|===

No corpus file carries GM System **Off** — that part of the original claim
holds; probe 47 and the new probe 49 remain the only files that send it.

=== Probe 49 — the sounding voice across GM System Off, now measured (`[M]`)

`artifacts/probes/49_gm_off_during_note.mid` (162 bytes), generated by
`p49_gm_off_during_note()` in `make_probes.py`, targets the sounding-voice-
across-GM-System-Off question left `[O]` above. It holds a GS Sine Wave note
(key 60, vel 100) from 0.6 s to 3.6 s and lands GM System Off at 2.1 s,
squarely inside the sustain, then runs three controls: a silent post-Off
attempt at 4.2 s, a GM System On re-acquire and attempt at 6.3 s (which
sounds as Square Wave, because System On clears GS mode), and a closing GS
Reset plus Sine Wave attempt at 8.4 s. A reference render now exists —
`probe-results/49.flac`, 10.2917 s, 44100 Hz stereo.

**Alignment.** Probe 49's reference runs 0.3221 s ahead of the nominal MIDI
clock, measured from first-nonzero at each of the three sounding cases:

[options="header"]
|===
| Case | File time (first nonzero) | Nominal | Offset
| 1 | 0.27762 | 0.600 | 0.32238
| 3 | 5.97755 | 6.300 | 0.32245
| 4 | 8.07871 | 8.400 | 0.32129
|===

a 1.2 ms spread.

**Confirms probe 47 independently.** Sounding spans (file time):
0.27762-1.72512 (case 1), 5.97755-6.99277 (case 3), 8.07871-9.08528 (case 4).
The case-2 window (nominal 4.2-5.2 s) contains 97,020 samples, every one of
them exactly integer zero — bit-exact silence on a second, independently
captured reference file, confirming probe 47's case 2 result rather than
merely repeating it.

Harmonic content (200 ms Hann FFT from onset+0.3 s, dB relative to the
261.63 Hz fundamental):

[options="header"]
|===
| Case | 2nd | 3rd | 4th | 5th | 7th | Reads as
| 1 | −45.1 | −51.8 | −58.8 | −61.5 | −67.2 | Sine Wave
| 3 | −12.3 | −3.0 | −14.3 | −4.5 | −8.7 | Square Wave
| 4 | −45.0 | −51.8 | −58.8 | −61.5 | −67.2 | Sine Wave
|===

These match probe 47's corresponding cases to 0.1 dB on every harmonic, so
probe 49's three controls all behaved as its docstring predicted.

**What happens to the sounding voice (the `[M]` this probe was built for).**
The held note is cut, not left ringing: it sounds from nominal 0.600 to
nominal 2.0473 and then stops. No noise burst, no garbage, no partial-sample
truncation artefact beyond the click characterised in xref:#item33[Item 33]
below. The GM System Off is scheduled at nominal 2.100, so the cut lands
52.7 ms early.

**The refcount question stays `[O]`.** Whether a voice already sounding when
GM System Off arrives holds a reference of its own and so survives the
release (`[A:0x16066]`) is NOT settled by this probe. As reasoned above, the
voice is cut under both hypotheses — the release call and the shared reset
tail's `ResetAllChannelControllers` both reclaim it, and the only window in
which the two hypotheses would sound different is shorter than one render
buffer. This probe bounded the question exactly as its docstring predicted
(a clean, characterised cut with no anomaly beyond the click) and did not
settle it. The `[O]` stands.

**Third data point for the early-SysEx caveat.** Our engine's earliness on
any non-note-on event is bounded by construction at one service block: the
render loop anchors service blocks to the absolute sample position and
dispatches every event due inside a block before rendering a sample of it,
using each event's true sub-block offset only for a note-on via
`voice_set_event_offset`
(`src/engine/smf.c:456-473`, offset consumed at line 470, cleared at line
474). That block is `SERVICE_BLOCK_FRAMES`, `256u * RESAMPLE_FACTOR` with
`RESAMPLE_FACTOR` shipping at 1 (`src/engine/voice.h:19,37`) — 256 frames,
11.61 ms at the engine's 22050 Hz render rate. Our engine cuts at nominal
2.0904 against the SysEx's nominal 2.100 — 9.6 ms early, inside that
11.61 ms bound. The reference cuts 52.7 ms early here; probe 48 measured
36.5 ms early on a different file — about 4.5x and 3.1x that bound. If the
real driver also applies the SysEx at a service-block boundary, its block
would have to be several times larger, or it drains several blocks ahead;
that is a lead for a future pass, not a finding. The reference's note-ons in
these same files stay accurate to about 1 ms. So the reference's
SysEx-effect earliness is now measured at 36.5 ms and 52.7 ms on two files,
still unexplained and still not attributable between "the driver applies the
reset out-of-band" and "the capture chain delivered the SysEx early".

[#item33]
=== Item 33 — the reference's GM-System-Off cut passes through a ~68 us transition; our engine's is a single-sample step. OPEN

Probe 49's held Sine Wave note gives the first per-sample look at the
GM-System-Off cut itself (as opposed to the ≤2 ms-resolution USE RHYTHM PART
cut in xref:#item32[Item 32]).

**Reference, at the capture's native 44100 Hz.** The transition is at file
t=1.724286. Consecutive samples through it: 3698, 4476, 5363, 5171, 3387,
981, -499, -513, 109, 335, 38. Largest single-sample step there is 2406 LSB
= 24.0% of the tone's steady amplitude (10012 LSB), against a natural largest
step of 390 LSB (3.9%) during the steady tone — a genuine discontinuity,
about 6x the largest step the waveform ever makes on its own. That is the
click.

A gain envelope recovered by least-squares-fitting the tone before the cut
(261.41 Hz, fit residual 1.0% of amplitude) and dividing the actual samples
by the extrapolated sine gives gain ~1.00 up to the transition, then 0.635,
0.174, then ~0 — the collapse spans roughly 3 samples at 44100 Hz (~68 us),
not a slow fade. Contrast, same file: a normal note-off (case 4, nominal
9.4 s) decays over about 40 ms — 10008 LSB peak at file 9.0453 falling to
266 at 9.0813 and to zero at 9.0853. So the ~68 us collapse is specific to
the reset cut and is not some blanket property of the capture chain.

**Measurement ceiling.** The reference captures are band-limited to
11025 Hz: in probe 49's case 3 (the harmonically rich Square Wave, the most
demanding test), 99.9997% of the energy sits below 10500 Hz and every band
above 11025 Hz is 85-97 dB down. Probe 47's case 3 measures the same. That
means the real synth renders at 22050 Hz and these 44100 Hz captures are
upsampled, so the exact per-sample values at a transition cannot be
recovered without assuming the upsampler's filter — a capture-ceiling fact
that bounds what any per-sample claim from these references can mean, and in
particular bounds the ratio below.

**Like-for-like at the synth's native 22050 Hz.** Decimating the reference
by 2 (both phases, to bracket the unknown upsampler phase) and comparing
against our engine, rendered natively at 22050 Hz:

[options="header"]
|===
| Rate / source | Consecutive samples near the transition | Largest step | vs steady amplitude | Natural largest step (steady tone)
| 44100 Hz, reference | 3698, 4476, 5363, 5171, 3387, 981, -499, -513, 109, 335, 38 | 2406 LSB | 24.0% | 3.9%
| 22050 Hz, reference (phase 0) | ..., 3192, 3532, 4476, 5171, 981, -513, 335, -213, ... | 4190 LSB | 41.8% | 7.7%
| 22050 Hz, reference (phase 1) | ..., 3533, 3698, 5363, 3387, -499, 109, 38, -98, ... | 3886 LSB | 38.8% | 7.8%
| 22050 Hz, our engine (native) | 6287, 6048, 5778, 5478, 0, 0, 0, 0 | 5478 LSB | 77.5% | 7.8%
|===

Our engine goes from full amplitude to exact zero in ONE sample. The
reference passes through an intermediate sample and then a small decaying
tail over a few more. Our largest step is 77.5% of amplitude against the
reference's 38.8-41.8% — roughly twice the step, about +6 dB more impulsive.
The DIRECTION is robust (both decimation phases agree); the exact ratio is
NOT pinned, because of the upsampling ceiling above.

**Verdict.** The click is real and is genuine reference behaviour, not a
slopgs defect; our engine reproduces it and overshoots it by roughly a
factor of two in step size.

**Status: OPEN, no fix attempted.** A fix means a one-to-two-sample gain
ramp in the voice teardown — a render-loop change needing maintainer review —
and it is not being made here.

'''

[#item26]
== 2026-07-29 — The amplitude/pitch EG runs on a 96 dB normalized scale, so the release traverses 96 dB, not 100 — `[A:0x1685e]`,`[A:0x18a7a]`. SHIPPED

Found while chasing xref:#item21-addendum-doomdrum[Item 21's doomdrum
addendum]. `tests/doomdrum.mid`'s tails decayed measurably faster than the
reference on every hit, not only on the retriggered one that entry named.

**What the EG's numbers mean.** Table C (`0x1a9d8`, 201 × int16, built by
`0x1685e`–`0x168b7`) is not a shape LUT. Replaying the builder with the exact
`.rdata` float32 constants — `0x11d04` = 1/200, `0x11d00` = 10000.0,
`0x11cfc` = 1/96, `0x11ce8` = 1000.0 — gives

----
t[0] = 0
t[i] = trunc(1000 + log10((i/200)^2) * 10000 * (1/96))       // i = 1..200
     = trunc(1000 * (1 + 20*log10(i/200) / 96))
----

which is a **linear-amplitude ratio → normalized-dB position** converter over
exactly **96 dB**: `1000` is 0 dB, `0` is −96 dB. (`fldlg2`/`fyl2x` at
`0x16880`–`0x16884` is the `log10` that
xref:SPEC.adoc#t4[SPEC.adoc T.4]'s transcription of this formula had dropped;
the published table values are unaffected and were reproduced exactly.)

**Where that scale is used.** `0x18a7a`, the EG evaluator both release
configurators call, returns a level in those units on every branch `[A]`:

[options="header"]
|===
| branch | VMA | returned level
| attack | `0x18ad3`–`0x18b24` | `TableC[(elapsed*1000/attackDur) / 5]` — the conversion into the dB scale
| decay | `0x18b4a`–`0x18b8b` | `1000 - (1000-sustain)*elapsed/decayDur`, already in the scale
| sustain | `0x18c0b` | the stored `+0x1c` sustain permille verbatim
| release | `0x18c46`–`0x18c7f` | `L0 * (relDur - elapsed) / relDur`, a straight line from the level at release onset down to `0`, i.e. to −96 dB
|===

So every EG segment is a straight line on a 96 dB scale, and a release that
reaches zero in `relDur` traverses `96 * L0/1000` dB in that time.

**Consequence for the release rate.** `0x197dc` sets
`relDur = relDur * L0 / 1000` before the segment runs (see
xref:#item21-resolved-2[the next entry]), so the *rate* is a constant
`96 dB / authoredRelease`, independent of level. `src/engine/voice.c`'s
`exp_coef_scaled` used `10^(-5/samples)`, i.e. **100 dB** over the authored
release — 4.2% too fast. This is the same 96 dB that
xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-4-3[S2.4.3]'s probe-35 decay measurement had already
found empirically as "96.5 dB over `seconds`, `[O]` whether it is the 96.33
dB 16-bit floor". It is neither fitted nor the 16-bit floor: it is 96 exactly,
by construction, and it applies to the release as well as the decay.

**Measured, `tests/doomdrum.mid`.** Per-hit least-squares fit of the 50 ms
RMS envelope in dB over +0.30 s…+1.50 s after each of the eight hits,
reference vs. render, after `score.py`'s own alignment:

[options="header"]
|===
| hit (s) | ref dB/s | 100 dB build | 96 dB build
| 0.00 | −35.19 | −37.31 | −35.85
| 2.18 | −35.16 | −36.96 | −35.51
| 4.36 | −35.12 | −36.83 | −35.39
| 6.54 | −35.20 | −37.40 | −35.95
| 8.72 | −34.05 | −35.00 | −33.78
| 10.91 | −34.10 | −34.83 | −33.61
| 13.09 | −34.26 | −35.29 | −34.07
| 15.27 | −34.04 | −35.00 | −33.77
|===

Mean absolute slope error 1.43 → 0.51 dB/s. Corpus gate: `doomdrum` −13.66 →
−14.06 dB with its invented-silence column 1680 → 1155 ms, corpus mean
−23.39 → −23.41 dB, and `warm-echo` −38.89 → −38.76 dB (the only item that
moves the wrong way, by 0.13 dB).

**Not fixed by this.** The same measurement shows a level offset of
−4.1…−5.3 dB in `doomdrum`'s tail that the slope correction does not touch;
see xref:#item31[Item 31].

'''

[#item21-resolved-2]
== 2026-07-29 — Item 21's remaining step, the level-scaled fast release, is `[A:0x197dc]`/`[A:0x19834]` and reduces to one line. SHIPPED — but it does NOT unblock the top-up cadence, and the reason given for expecting it to was wrong

xref:#item21-reap[Item 21's last addendum] closed with "implementing that
level scaling is the remaining step on this item". It is implemented here.

**The two configurators.** `0x197dc` (ordinary note-off) and `0x19834`
(fast/choke) share their first 0x43 bytes verbatim:

----
// 0x197dc __thiscall ConfigureSegment(seg, targetLo, targetHi)
// 0x19834 __thiscall ReconfigureSegment(seg, targetLo, targetHi, sampleRate)
L = EvalEnvelope(seg, target, &seg->0x28, curve = 1);   // 0x18a7a
seg->relDur = seg->relDur * L / 1000;   // 0x1049c multiply, 0x10490 divide

if (this is 0x19834) {                  // 0x19877 onward, absent from 0x197dc
    clamp = (unsigned)sampleRate / 70;              // 0x1987f, 0x19882
    if (seg->relDur > clamp) seg->relDur = clamp;   // 0x1988a - 0x1989f
}

seg->0x28/0x2c = target;                // release-onset timestamp
----

`L` is on the 96 dB scale of xref:#item26[Item 26], so `relDur = authored *
L/1000` makes the *rate* level-independent — which an exponential release
already reproduces. **On the ordinary note-off path there was therefore
nothing to implement**; the level scaling is only observable through
``0x19834``'s clamp, which caps the *time to silence* rather than the rate.
In rate terms the whole mechanism collapses to

----
fast_release_seconds = min(authored_release_seconds, FAST_RELEASE_S / L)
----

with `L` the voice's level as a fraction of the 96 dB span. A quiet voice
gets a *slower* ramp (it has less distance to cover in the same 14.29 ms),
never a faster one, and the patch's own release is the floor on the rate.

**The cadence claim in xref:#item21-reap[Item 21's addendum] was wrong.**
That entry expected level scaling to let the driver's true once-per-block
top-up cadence hold, because "Branch B's comparator picks the QUIETEST
released voice first, so the voices it marks drain in a fraction of the
clamp". Measured directly, with `TOPUP_INTERVAL_FRAMES` at the true
`256*RESAMPLE_FACTOR` cadence:

[options="header"]
|===
| build | `--selftest` saturation | corpus mean
| before, topup 2048 | 48/48 | −23.39
| before, topup 256 | **43/48** | −23.39
| level-scaled, topup 2048 | 48/48 | −23.40
| level-scaled, topup 256 | **43/48** | −23.40
|===

Identical. The saturation test holds all 80 notes, so Branch B never has a
released voice to prefer and every victim it marks is at full level, where
the clamp dominates and the scaling is a no-op. The cadence gap is not a
drain-time problem and is not this item's; `TOPUP_INTERVAL_FRAMES` stays at
its fitted 2048 and the cadence remains **`[O]`**.

**Item 21 is closed.** The level scaling ships (it is `[A]`, it costs one
line, and it is the correct model even though this corpus cannot see it);
the top-up cadence is tracked separately from here on.

'''

[#item27]
== 2026-07-29 — Decay key-follow divides by **127**, not 128, and the key-follow term alone is clamped to ±4800 cents — `[A:0x1992a]`,`[A:0x18e2c]`. SHIPPED

xref:SPEC.adoc#s2-4-3[S2.4.3]'s runtime decay key-follow carried the
normalization as `[I]` from the DLS-1 spec ("key/128 … the driver's own
consumption code is unrecovered"), with probe 35 measuring the divisor as
126.5 ± 0.8 and explicitly unable to separate 127 from 128. The consumption
code is `0x198b6`, the note-setup segment configurator, and it settles both
questions.

`0x198b6` copies the articulation block's 32-byte EG record into the voice's
segment object (`rep movsd`, 8 dwords, `0x198df`) and then rescales two of
the three durations:

----
// attack, 0x198e1 - 0x19918
cents = (int16)seg->0x18 * (WORD)velocity / 127;      // imul, then idiv 0x7f
seg->attackDur = seg->attackDur * Pow2Cents(cents) / 4096;

// decay, 0x1991a - 0x19952
cents = (int16)seg->0x1a * (WORD)key / 127;           // imul, then idiv 0x7f
seg->decayDur  = seg->decayDur  * Pow2Cents(cents) / 4096;

// decay-to-sustain rescale, 0x19953 - 0x19984 (already in SPEC.adoc)
seg->decayDur  = seg->decayDur * (1000 - (int16)seg->0x1c) / 1000;
----

`idiv 0x7f` is 127, and it truncates toward zero. `Pow2Cents` is `0x18e1c`,
which clamps its argument to `[-4800, +4800]` (`0x18e2c` against `0x12c0`,
`0x18e63` against `0xffffed40`) before evaluating — so the clamp is on the
**key-follow term alone**, not on the summed duration.

The EG record's own layout follows from the same copy `[A]`:

[options="header"]
|===
| offset | size | field
| `+0x00` | 8 | attack duration, samples
| `+0x08` | 8 | decay duration, samples
| `+0x10` | 8 | release duration, samples
| `+0x18` | WORD | attack scaling depth, cents at full velocity
| `+0x1a` | WORD | decay scaling depth, cents at key 127
| `+0x1c` | WORD | sustain level, permille on the 96 dB scale
| `+0x20` | 8 | segment start timestamp
| `+0x28` | 8 | release-onset timestamp (`0x7fffffff_ffffffff` until released)
|===

Effect is small — for `gm.dls`'s median `−3979` depth the two divisors differ
by 0.8% of a duration — and the corpus registers it as noise: mean −23.41 →
−23.40 dB, `doomdrum` −14.06 → −14.02 dB. It ships because it is `[A]`
against an `[I]`, not because it measures better.

'''

[#item28]
== 2026-07-29 — `0x18e1c` reads `0x1ad00`/`0x1af58`, not `0x1a9d8`; it is a 2^(x/1200) evaluator and the velocity→attack term it feeds is LIVE, not inert — `[A:0x18e1c]`,`[A:0x16700]`. SHIPPED

xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-1-2[S5.1.2]'s parenthetical read `TableLookup_0x18e1c` as
"reads the 256-entry WORD table at `0x1a9d8`, which is **all-zero** in this
build `[A]`, so the velocity term above is `0`", and extended the same
conclusion to `0x1a7d8`. Every part of that is wrong.

`0x18e1c` reads two DWORD tables, `0x1ad00` and `0x1af58`, and evaluates
`2^(cents/1200)` in 1/4096 fixed point by splitting the cents `[A]`:

----
cents = clamp(cents, -4800, +4800);              // 0x18e2c, 0x18e63
oct   = (cents / 100) / 12;
semi  = (cents / 100) % 12;
frac  =  cents % 100;
return (tab1200[frac] << oct) * tab12[semi] >> 12;   // 0x18e44 - 0x18e59
----

`tab1200` (`0x1ad00`, indices −100…100) and `tab12` (`0x1af58`, indices
−48…48) are built at `0x16700`–`0x167ab` as `trunc(pow(2.0, i/1200)*4096)`
and `trunc(pow(2.0, i/12)*4096)` — `0x11cd0` = 2.0, `0x11cd4` = 1200.0,
`0x11cc8` = 12.0, `0x11ccc` = 4096.0.

**"All-zero on disk" proves nothing about any of these tables.** All of them
live in `PAGEDATA` and are built at driver init: `0x1a9d8` by `0x1685e`
(xref:#item26[Item 26]), `0x1ad00`/`0x1af58` by `0x16700`, and `0x1a7d8` by
`0x1691e`–`0x1695c`, which is a 256-entry `fsin` table scaled by `0x11ce4` =
100.0 — the LFO sine xref:SPEC.adoc#t5[T.5] already documents, not an inert
duplicate of anything.

So the velocity→EG1-attack connection (`usSource=2` → `usDestination=0x0206`)
is fully live, applied exactly as the decay key-follow is:
`attackDur *= 2^((depth * velocity / 127) / 1200)`. **27 of ``gm.dls``'s 235
instruments carry a non-zero one** `[D:gm.dls]`, so this is not a theoretical
path. Implemented in `src/engine/dls.c` (`eg1_attack_vel_tc`) and
`src/engine/voice.c` (`scale_tc_by_source`).

Corpus is unmoved (mean −23.40 dB, every item unchanged): none of the seven
`tests/` items uses one of the 27 instruments. It is live and verified
separately — renders of `artifacts/probes/01_programs.mid`, which sweeps
programs, differ with and without it.

**Still `[O]`:** the matching `usSource=2` → `0x030a` (velocity → EG2
attack), 8 connections in `gm.dls`, reaching the pitch envelope through the
same `0x198b6` call on `voice+0x38`. Not implemented.

'''

[#item29]
== 2026-07-29 — `voice+0x104` is the DEVICE object, not the articulation block; `0x19834`'s clamp argument is the sample rate and its segment durations are in samples — `[A:0x12b3e]`,`[A:0x12fea]`

Three statements in `SPEC.adoc` follow from a single mis-identification.

`0x19aa4` passes ``0x19834``'s fourth argument as `[[voice+0x104]]`
(`0x19ac7`, `0x19b1b`). `voice+0x104` is written at `0x19ba7` from
`SetupNote`'s **first** argument, and `SetupNote`'s only call site,
`0x12fea`, passes the device object `esi`. That object's `+0x0` is written
`0x5622` = **22050** at `0x12b3e`, in the same constructor that sets the
pool's `+0xf50` = 0x30 and `+0xf52` = 6 `[A]`.

Corrections:

* xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-6[S5.6]'s pseudocode passes `region->0x0`. It is
  `device->sampleRate`.
* xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-12[S5.12] / xref:SPEC.adoc#o12[O12] state "the object
  pointed to by voice `+0x104` is the articulation block: `+0x40` is
  LFO_FREQUENCY and `+0x20` onward is the EG1 block". Those offsets are real
  but belong to a *different* `SetupNote` argument, `[ebp+0xc]`, which
  `0x19c45`/`0x19c50`/`0x19c9d` pass as `artic`, `artic+0x20` and
  `artic+0x40`. `voice+0x104` is the device: `+0x0` sample rate, `+0x4`
  and `+0x8` configuration flags read at `0x19be1`/`0x19bea`, `+0xc` read at
  `0x19b72`, `+0x18`/`+0x1c` a 64-bit ceiling clamped onto the voice's
  next-event time at `0x19cc7`. O12 as written is void; what remains open is
  the device object's layout beyond those five offsets.
* xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-12[S5.12]'s open item "the numeric time unit of
  ``0x19834``'s divisor constant `70`" is **closed**. The divisor divides the
  sample rate, so it is dimensionless and the segment durations it is
  compared against are in samples: 22050/70 = **315 samples = 14.29 ms**.
  This is the reading `src/engine/voice.c`'s `FAST_RELEASE_S` already ships;
  it now has a second, independent derivation.

The measured 70.0 ms full-silence figure in xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-6[S5.6]
`[M: probe 18]` is **not** reconciled by this and is not evidence for a
70 ms clamp: it is 4.9× the instruction-level value. That probe's choke
falls are contaminated by the choking note sounding through them (recorded
in xref:#item21-reap[Item 21's addendum]), so the discrepancy stands as a
measurement to redo, not as a conflict in the disassembly.

'''

[#item30]
== 2026-07-29 — `0x19aa4`'s cheap/full path test is inverted in S5.6's pseudocode — `[A:0x19aaf]`

xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-6[S5.6] renders `ScheduleFastRelease` as taking the cheap
path (reapply the existing target through the clamped configurator) when
`voice->0x134 != 0 || voice->0x140 != 0`. The instructions read the other
way `[A]`:

----
voice->0x138 = 1;                                  // 0x19ab6, unconditional
if (voice->0x134 != 0) goto full;                  // 0x19aaf, 0x19abc
if (voice->0x140 != 0) goto full;                  // 0x19abe, 0x19ac5
// both zero: cheap path at 0x19ac7
----

Both fields are zeroed by ordinary note-off (`0x19a2c`), so the cheap path is
the *already-released* case — re-clamping a voice that is mid-release — and
the full path is the still-held case. `SPEC.adoc`
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-8-2[S3.8.2] already renders the same routine correctly, so
this was an internal inconsistency between two passages describing one
function, not a uniformly wrong reading. The corrected form is what
`src/engine/voice.c` already implements by collapsing the two paths, so
nothing in the engine changes; the pseudocode does.

'''

[#item31]
== 2026-07-29 — `tests/doomdrum.mid`: the drum tail sits ~4.6 dB below the reference at a matched decay rate — OPEN

What is left of xref:#item21-addendum-doomdrum[the doomdrum addendum] after
xref:#item26[Item 26] fixes the slope. The addendum attributed the
mismatch to the 8.929 s cymbal same-note retrigger; it is not that. The
deficit is present after **every** hit in the file, including the five that
contain no cymbal at all.

**Measured.** After `score.py` alignment, per-hit 50 ms RMS envelopes:
the render sits −4.10…−5.31 dB below the reference at +0.4 s after each of
the eight hits, while the fitted decay slopes over +0.30…+1.50 s now agree
to 0.51 dB/s. The offset is a step, not an accumulation: it is fully
established by +0.10 s and flat thereafter.

**Isolated to one voice.** The tail is dominated by note 41 (Low Floor Tom,
`usKeyGroup` 0, looping region, sustain 0). Complex demodulation at its
70.24 Hz fundamental over +0.3…+1.0 s: the pitch matches to +2.1 cents and
the level is −4.9…−5.4 dB on all four measured hits, i.e. a pure amplitude
offset on that voice, not a detuning or a wrong sample. Band energy over
the same window: 60–120 Hz −5.14 dB, and the (55 dB quieter) 500–2000 Hz
region −11 dB.

**Where the step lands.** The render tracks the reference to +0.06 s, then
loses ~5 dB between +0.06 s and +0.10 s. The three note-offs of the first
hit are at +0.121/+0.143/+0.150 s, so the step is *before* the note-off, in
the decay segment, not at the release transition. The instrument's decay is
2885 tc with a −4786 key-follow, i.e. 1352 tc = 2.19 s at key 41, and
96 dB/2.19 s = 43.8 dB/s over 0.107 s is 4.7 dB — the size of the offset. The
reading that fits is that the reference's amplitude has barely decayed at
all by the time note-off lands, which none of the mechanisms in
xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-1-2[S5.1.2] predicts.

**Not yet excluded:** a delay before the decay segment starts; the wave-level
`wsmp` attenuation being combined differently from
xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-5[S3.5]'s "region overrides wave" for a region whose own
`lAttenuation` is 0 (key 41's is, key 36's is −25 dB); the looping region's
loop point. Ruled out: pitch, key-follow divisor
(xref:#item27[Item 27] moves it by 0.7% of a duration), and the release rate.

'''

[#item25-resolved]
== 2026-07-29 — Item 25 RESOLVED by `[A:0x136e1]`: GM System Off *does* clear GS mode; the shared tail has a third entry nobody recorded; `0x150bc` is not what S4.5 said it was

xref:#item25[Item 25] recorded a contradiction it could not decide without
the binary: xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5] claimed GM System Off both "calls
`ResetDevice`" and leaves the GS-mode flag "untouched", while
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-1[S4.6.1] listed `device+0xf54 = 0` among
``ResetDevice``'s unconditional writes. The binary was disassembled at the two
address ranges the entry nominated, plus the shared tail its secondary
observation flagged. The first of the two readings is correct, and chasing the
secondary observation turned up two further errors in the same passage.

=== The Universal Non-Realtime branch

`0x136d5`–`0x13705`:

[source]
----
if (buf[3] != 0x09) goto drop;          // 0x136d5, the only gate on the branch
ResetDevice(device);                    // 0x136e1  <-- BEFORE the sub-ID2 test
resetPrograms = 1;                      // 0x136e6/0x136e8, read by the tail
isSystemOn    = (buf[4] == 1);          // 0x136eb/0x136ee
if (isSystemOn)
    device.gsMode = 0;                  // 0x136f5, System On only
Collection(device+0x20, isSystemOn);    // 0x136fc/0x13700
goto sharedTail;                        // 0x13705 -> 0x137fa
----

The call to `ResetDevice` precedes the sub-ID2 test entirely; the sub-ID1
compare at `0x136d5` is the only gate on the branch. Both GM System On and GM
System Off reach it.

``ResetDevice``'s `0xf54` store is likewise unconditional. `0x123be`–`0x123d7`
is the function epilogue, entered only by the loop exit at `0x123bc`, and
contains no branch target:

[source]
----
// per-channel loop over 16 channels ends at 0x123bc
device.masterVolAtten = 0;              // 0x123be
device.gsMode         = 0;              // 0x123c5
device.rhythmPart[0]  = 1;              // 0x123ce
return;                                 // 0x123d7
----

**Therefore GM System Off clears the GS-mode flag.** xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]'s
"untouched" was true of instruction `0x136f5` alone, not of the message;
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-4[S4.6.4]'s "unchanged (System Off)" cell was wrong and
becomes `0`. Every other GM-System-Off cell in that table is inherited from
`ResetDevice`, which is genuinely called, so all of them stand unchanged.

A corollary: the dedicated clear at `0x136f5` on the System On path is **dead
code**. `ResetDevice` zeroed `0xf54` twelve instructions earlier, so it can
never observe a nonzero flag.

=== The shared tail: the gate is a register, and there is a third caller

xref:#item25[Item 25] flagged xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]'s claim that the tail
at `0x137fa` is always taken "because the gating flag was just set to `1` two
instructions earlier in **both callers**". Three things are wrong with that.

[source]
----
sharedTail:                                     // 0x137fa
    ResetAllChannelControllers(device);         // 0x13802, unconditional
    if (resetPrograms)                          // 0x13807, tests EBX
        ResetAllProgramsAndRhythmGroups(device);// 0x13813
----

First, the gate tests **EBX, not `device+0xf54`**. On the GS Reset path EBX is
set to `1` at `0x137f1`/`0x137f3` and then additionally *stored* to `0xf54` at
`0x137f4`, which is what made the register and the flag look like the same
thing. On the GM path EBX is set to `1` at `0x136e6`/`0x136e8`, where it also
serves as the constant `1` for the sub-ID2 compare, while `0xf54` is being
*cleared*. EBX survives the intervening calls: `0x150bc` pushes it at `0x150d7`
and pops at `0x151a1`; `0x126c2` pushes at `0x126ca`.

Second, there are **three** entries into the tail, not two. USE RHYTHM PART
jumps into it at `0x137b5`:

[source]
----
useRhythmPart:                                  // 0x137a2, address 40 1n 15
    if (device.gsMode == 0) goto drop;          // 0x137a2/0x137a9
    device.rhythmPart[n] = buf[8];              // 0x137ab/0x137ae
    goto sharedTail;                            // 0x137b5
----

Third, on that path the gate is **false**. EBX is zeroed at function entry
(`0x13683`) and the only writes to it anywhere on the Roland branch are in the
tuning-grid case at `0x1378d`/`0x13790`, which exits at `0x137a0` without ever
reaching the tail. So USE RHYTHM PART arrives at `0x137fa` with `EBX = 0`.

The consequence is not cosmetic. A USE RHYTHM PART message, once GS mode is
on, runs the whole of `ResetAllChannelControllers` (xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-2[S4.6.2]):
it releases every currently-active voice, cancels every pending node in all
six per-channel queues, and reschedules Modulation `0`, Pitch Bend `8192`,
Channel Volume `100`, Pan `64` and Expression `127` on **all sixteen
channels**, plus a sustain-release sentinel per channel. It then skips
`ResetAllProgramsAndRhythmGroups`, so Bank/Program survive. Neither
`SPEC.adoc` nor `CHART.adoc` recorded any of this; both described the message
as a single byte written to `device+0x130c+n`.

The original claim that the tail is "always taken" is therefore true of the
two callers xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5] knew about and false in general. Those
three are the only entries into `0x137fa`: two `jmp`s (`0x13705`, `0x137b5`)
and the fallthrough from `0x137f4`, with no data pointer to the address.

Two smaller things fall out of the same read. The System Reset (`0xFF`) path
clears `0xf54` at `0x131ee` immediately *before* its own `call 0x12354`, so
that clear is redundant for the same reason `0x136f5` is. And that path runs
the two reset functions in the **opposite order** to the shared tail: `0x13202`
calls `ResetAllProgramsAndRhythmGroups` and `0x1320f` then calls
`ResetAllChannelControllers`, where the tail calls `0x126c2` first and
`0x12780` second. Since `ResetAllChannelControllers` cancels every pending
queue node and `ResetAllProgramsAndRhythmGroups` schedules program/locale
events, the ordering decides whether the program events survive the cancel —
so the `0xFF` column of xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-4[S4.6.4] is not simply the union
of the two functions' writes. Tracing which events actually survive each
ordering was not done; it is `[O]` and belongs to a separate item.

=== `0x150bc` is the `gm.dls` acquire/release, not a Master Volume call

xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]'s closing paragraph described `0x150bc` as "the
extra Master-Volume-related call ... that only the `0xFF`/System-Reset path
makes (`0x13218`)". Both halves are wrong. It takes one boolean and is the
acquire/release for the DLS collection:

[source]
----
Collection(obj, wantLoaded):                        // 0x150bc
    if (obj.loaded == wantLoaded) return;           // 0x150cc, no-op early-out
    if (wantLoaded) {
        obj.collection = Open("\\SystemRoot\\System32\\Drivers\\gm.dls");
                                                    // 0x150e6, name at 0x1506e
        obj.slot[0] = NewInstrument(obj.collection, DRUM_BIT);
                                                    // 0x15104/0x15107
        for (i = 1; i < 16; i++)                    // 0x15119-0x15133
            obj.slot[i] = NewInstrument(obj.collection, 0);
        obj.loaded = 1;                             // 0x15135
    } else {
        for (i = 0; i < 16; i++) {                  // 0x1513e-0x15187
            Release(obj.slot[i]);     obj.slot[i].locale     = NONE;
            Release(obj.prevSlot[i]); obj.prevSlot[i].locale = NONE;
        }
        Release(obj.collection);                    // 0x15193 -> 0x16048
        obj.collection = 0;                         // 0x15198
        obj.loaded     = 0;                         // 0x1519c
    }
----

The slots are 8-byte `(handle, locale)` pairs: the live array at `obj+0x2c`,
the parallel "previous instrument" array `0x80` bytes above at `obj+0xac`.
`NONE` is the sentinel `0x40000000`, `DRUM_BIT` is `0x80000000`, `obj.loaded`
is `obj+0x18`, `obj.collection` is `obj+0x28`.

All four call sites, arguments read from the binary:

[options="header"]
|===
| Site | Argument | Effect
| `0x12b4c` (device constructor) | `edi = 1`, set at `0x12af6`/`0x12af8` | **acquire** — this is where `gm.dls` is loaded, at device open
| `0x13218` (MIDI System Reset `0xFF`) | `edi = 1`, set at `0x131d7`/`0x131d9` | acquire; no-op when already loaded
| `0x13700` (GM System On/Off) | `eax = (sub-ID2 == 1)` | System On: acquire (normally a no-op). **System Off: RELEASE**
| `0x15323` (teardown) | literal `0` | release
|===

`0x150bc` is the only site in the binary that opens the collection — confirmed
by disassembling every section and grepping for the target, and by confirming
its address appears in no data section as a function pointer.

**Net effect: GM System Off tears down the instrument collection.** With
`obj.collection` null and `obj.loaded` zero, `0x14f5e` (the per-slot
instrument-handle setter) returns failure at `0x14f6f` and `0x151b0`
short-circuits at `0x151c8`, so no new handle can be created and Program
Change stops having any audible effect. `FindInstrument` (`0x14800`) walks the
collection list at `obj+0x0` — the same list `0x164ee` links into and
`0x16048` unlinks from — so its scan finds nothing and the note-on path
exhausts its three retries into the no-voice exit at `0x1302a`. Only a
subsequent GM System On or MIDI System Reset (`0xFF`) re-acquires.

The state teardown is `[A]`. The audible consequence — silence until
re-acquire — is `[I]`: `0x16048` unlinks only once the `InterlockedDecrement`
on the collection's refcount at `+0x2c` (`0x16066`, import thunk `0x11874`)
reaches zero, and whether a currently-sounding voice holds a reference of its
own was not traced. It enters `SPEC.adoc` as `[I]` and needs an `[M]` probe.

=== The falsification probe from Item 25 needs replacing

xref:#item25[Item 25] proposed: GS Reset, CC0 `= 8`, GM System Off, Program
Change 80, note — expecting Sine Wave (bank 8) if GS mode survived and Square
Wave (bank 0) if it did not. Under the confirmed reading that probe produces
**silence** either way, because the same message released the collection. It
cannot distinguish the two readings.

Three probes replace it, none generated yet — worth adding to
`make_probes.py` when a reference render can be captured:

. GS Reset, CC0 `= 8`, GM System **On**, Program Change 80, note. Uses the
  acquiring form of the message, so it isolates the GS-mode question from the
  teardown. Square Wave confirms GS mode was cleared.
. GS Reset, note, GM System Off, note. Tests the teardown directly; the second
  note silent confirms the `[I]` above.
. GS Reset, sustained chord across several channels with Volume/Pan/Expression
  moved off their defaults, then USE RHYTHM PART on an unrelated part. Every
  note cutting off and every controller snapping back confirms the tail entry.

=== What shipped in the engine, and what did not

`src/engine/synth.c`:

* GM System Off already cleared GS mode, because the engine collapses all
  three reset functions into `synth_reset()` and that zeroes `g_gs_mode`
  before the sub-ID2 test is reached — the same accident of ordering the
  driver has. The sub-ID2-gated clear that sat after it was dead code against
  its own reset, exactly as `0x136f5` is; removed, with the comment that
  claimed System Off "leaves untouched" corrected. **No behavioural change.**
* USE RHYTHM PART now calls a new `reset_all_channel_controllers_device()`.
  This is distinct from the existing CC121 helper, which is gated by
  `CC121_RESETS_VOLUME` and runs on one channel: the tail's version resets
  Channel Volume and runs on all sixteen. **Behavioural change**, verified on
  `probes/48_rhythm_part_reset.mid`: sounding voices cut at the message, and a
  retrigger with no intervening controller returns 28× louder and centred —
  which is exactly `(100·127)² / (40·60)²` for volume `40`→`100` and
  expression `60`→`127` under the squared law, so the reset is landing on the
  right fields and not merely making noise. Corpus mean unmoved at
  `r = 0.913` / `−23.39 dB`; no corpus item carries either message.
  **Superseded 2026-07-29** -- see
  xref:#item25-followup-gm-off-measured[today's follow-up entry]'s corpus
  SysEx census: this no longer holds, a proper SysEx parse finds ten corpus
  files carrying GM System On and/or USE RHYTHM PART (GM System Off itself
  is still carried by none, so that half of the original claim stands).

**Not implemented: the collection release** (xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-9-1[S4.9.1]).
GM System Off does not unload anything in the engine. The teardown is `[A]`
but its audible consequence is `[I]`, and shipping silence on a message some
files send as a politeness would be a bad trade against an unmeasured claim.
`probes/47_gm_off_state.mid` exists to settle it; this stays unimplemented
until a reference render says otherwise.
**Superseded 2026-07-29** -- see
xref:#item25-followup-gm-off-measured[today's follow-up entry]: the
reference render arrived, the audible consequence is now `[M]`, and the
release shipped.

=== What changed

* xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]: GM System Off row (clears GS mode via
  `ResetDevice`); GM System On row (the redundant clear); USE RHYTHM PART row
  (the tail entry); the closing converging-triggers paragraph, replaced in
  full — tail callers and gate, and ``0x150bc``'s identity and call sites.
* xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-3[S4.6.3]: ``0x151b0``'s `[O]` retired; its internal
  effect is now read.
* xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-4[S4.6.4]: GS-mode row, "GM On/Off net effect" cell
  `unchanged (System Off)` → `0`.
* xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-10[S4.10]: the `0x151b0` open item removed.
* `impl-chart/CHART.adoc`: the GM System Off and USE RHYTHM PART rows, and the
  Bank Select warning that lists how GS mode is left.

'''

[#item21-addendum-doomdrum]
== 2026-07-29 — Item 21 addendum: `tests/doomdrum.mid` reproduces the still-open level-scaling gap on a cymbal same-note retrigger -- OPEN, no fix attempted

Fresh repro for xref:item21-reap[Item 21]'s closing line ("Implementing that
level scaling is the remaining step on this item"), found via the
`dist/compare-field.html` spectrogram tool on `tests/doomdrum.flac` vs a
slopgs render, not by a corpus sweep.

**Symptom.** 8.60-9.75s window: residual -13.7 dB, envelope `r` 0.917, pair
rms -22.8 dB (reference) / -24.1 dB (slopgs). A visibly darker patch in the
slopgs spectrogram sits at roughly 8.8-9.0s, inside that window; the
same sub-window read alone (8.614-9.764s slopgs / 8.595-9.746s reference)
measures Δ rms -1.5 dB, Δ peak -1.0 dB against the reference.

**Cause, traced in `tests/doomdrum.mid`.** ch9 fires note 49 (Crash Cymbal
1, vel 71) and note 57 (Crash Cymbal 2, vel 61) together at t=8.721s, then
retriggers both, vel 23, at t=8.929s -- 208 ms later, while the first hit is
still ringing. Both crash regions carry `usKeyGroup==0` (checked directly
against `dist/gm.dls`'s standard-kit region table), so key-group choke
(SPEC.adoc xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-8-1[S3.8.1]) never applies here. What fires
instead is the same-note retrigger scan in `voice_note_on`
(`src/engine/voice.c:1070`-`1075`): a note-on for a (channel, note) already
sounding fast-releases the prior voice through the same `start_release(v,
1)` path as the key-group choke and the reserve top-up.

That fast-release is where Item 21's open gap bites: `FAST_RELEASE_S`
(`src/engine/voice.c:301`-`303`, 14.29 ms) clamps the segment uniformly
regardless of the voice's envelope level at the moment of retrigger, where
the driver's `0x19834` clamps a duration `0x197dc` has ALREADY scaled by
that level (xref:item21-reap[Item 21 addendum, "reap"]). The loud
(vel 71) crash retriggered only 208 ms in is exactly the case that gap
predicts will drain differently from the reference -- and the darker patch
lands within a few tens of ms of the 8.929s retrigger.

**Status:** corroborating evidence only. No fix attempted here; Item 21's
remaining step (level-scaling the fast-release clamp before applying it) is
unchanged.

'''

[#item25]
== 2026-07-28 — GM System Off cannot both call `ResetDevice` and leave the GS-mode flag untouched -- OPEN, not resolved here

**Path:** `SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5] (SysEx table, GM System Off row) and
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-4[S4.6.4] (reset-state summary, GS-mode row) vs.
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-1[S4.6.1] (`ResetDevice`'s own field table).

xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]'s GM System Off row (`F0 7E <any> 09 02 F7`) states both
things at once: "Calls `ResetDevice`" and "GS-mode flag **untouched**",
the latter cited to `[A:0x136f3]` (`je 0x136fc` skips the dedicated clear
when sub-ID2 `!= 1`). But xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-1[S4.6.1] lists
`device+0xf54` (GS-mode flag) `= 0` among ``ResetDevice``'s two
device-global writes, cited to `[A:0x123c5]` and described as
unconditional. If `ResetDevice` really is called on the Off path, the flag
is already zero by the time the skipped clear at `0x136f5` would have run,
and "untouched" is true only of that one instruction, not of the message.

The two readings cannot both stand:

* If `[A:0x123c5]` is unconditional and `ResetDevice` is called on the
  sub-ID2 `== 2` path, then GM System Off **does** clear GS mode, and
  xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-4[S4.6.4]'s "unchanged (System Off)" cell is wrong —
  that is a *net-effect* column, so it must account for the callee's
  writes, not just the caller's.
* If GM System Off does **not** clear GS mode, then `ResetDevice` is not
  actually called on that path (or its `0xf54` store is gated), and
  xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]'s "Calls `ResetDevice`" is the wrong half —
  which would in turn put every other GM-System-Off cell in
  xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-6-4[S4.6.4] (Volume 100, Pan 64, tuning grid zeroed, …)
  in doubt, since all of them are inherited from `ResetDevice`.

**Secondary observation, same passage.** xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5]'s
converging-triggers paragraph says the shared tail at `0x137fa` is always
taken "because the gating flag was just set to `1` two instructions earlier
in **both callers** `[A:0x13807]`". That holds for GS Reset (`0x137f4` sets
the flag) but not for GM System On, which *clears* it at `0x136f5`. Either
the tail's gate is not the GS-mode flag, or it is not always true on the
GM path. A future pass at these addresses should settle this at the same
time.

**Not resolved here, and no SPEC.adoc passage changed.** Deciding between
the readings needs the binary; this entry records the contradiction and
what would settle it, per the rule that SPEC.adoc carries no unresolved
alternatives.

**Where a future RE pass should look.** `0x136d5`–`0x136fc` (the
Universal Non-Realtime `09 xx` branch), specifically whether the
`call 0x12354` at `0x136e1` is reached on the sub-ID2 `== 2` path or only
on `== 1`; and `0x123be`–`0x123d7`, to confirm the `0xf54` store at
`0x123c5` sits outside any branch.

**Falsifiable without the binary.** GS Reset, then CC0 `= 8` on a channel,
then GM System Off, then Program Change 80 and a note. Bank 8 program 80 is
Sine Wave and bank 0 program 80 is Square Wave in `gm.dls`, so the two
readings are audibly distinct: a Sine Wave means GS mode survived GM System
Off, a Square Wave means it did not. Worth a probe in `make_probes.py` when
a reference render can be captured.

**Downstream.** `impl-chart/CHART.adoc` currently follows the reading
xref:SPEC.adoc#s4-5[S4.5] states explicitly (GM System Off leaves GS mode
as it was), in both its Bank Select warning and its SysEx table. If this
item resolves the other way, both passages need replacing.

'''

[#item21]
== 2026-07-28 — Ordinary-note-off release is too short against isolate references -- fitted floor SHIPPED, superseded 2026-07-28: the real defect is in the GAIN path, see the resolution addendum below

**Path:** `SPEC.adoc` xref:SPEC.adoc#s5-6[S5.6]/xref:SPEC.adoc#s3-8-2[S3.8.2] (the only documented minimum-release
mechanism -- the rate clamp at `0x19834`, dividing by literal `0x46`/70 --
is reachable only from the fast-release/choke path `0x19aa4`, never from
ordinary note-off `0x19a2c`) vs. this project's own direct measurement
against hand-cut excerpts.

**What I did:** added `RELEASE_FLOOR_S = 0.060` in `src/engine/voice.c`,
flooring the ordinary-note-off release SEGMENT DURATION (in seconds, before
conversion to a per-sample coefficient) at 60 ms. Gated `!fast`, so it never
touches the fast-release/choke path or `RELEASE_RATE_MULT` (left at 1.0).
Because SPEC.adoc's own rate-clamp mechanism cannot reach this path by its own
account, this floor has no cited disassembly counterpart and is `[F:fitted]`,
not `[A]`.

**Corpus sweep** (spectral-residual analysis, `compare_spectral_22050.overall_db`,
32 graded probes): 20 ms -9.7388, 40 ms
-9.7569, **60 ms -9.7693**, 90 ms -9.6624, against the no-floor baseline
-9.7206. 90 ms's apparent mean was contaminated by an alignment flip on
`31_tune_clamp_bend_sine` (a confirmed mis-lock, not a real regression) and
several probes had already turned around (started re-worsening) by that
point; 60 ms is the last point where every probe that moved was still
improving. Direct isolate measurement (reference time-to--20dB/-40dB after
note-off ~38/44 ms vs. our pre-fit 3.5/4.6 ms) independently corroborates a
value in the same 6-11x-stretch band. Full detail, including the isolate
measurement's own caveats (a confirmed piecewise capture-lag drift, and a
low-register resolution floor), is in `SPEC_LOG` xref:#entry10[Entry 10].

**Confirmed not to reproduce SPEC_LOG #15's prior failure mode:**
`field/corridor.mid` renders 1,159,168 frames untruncated at every floor
tested; `probes/04_envelope.mid` is byte-identical (`cmp`) up to a 40 ms
floor, since its own authored release (990 ms) sits far above any floor
tested.

**Genuinely open, flagged as a hypothesis only, not established:** the
reference isolate reaches -20 dB at ~38 ms but -40 dB at only ~44 ms -- a
~6 ms fall after a ~32 ms plateau. That shape is a DELAYED release onset,
not merely a slower release rate, and a pure duration floor on an
exponential release does not model it. A future RE pass should look at
SPEC.adoc xref:SPEC.adoc#part5[Part 5] `+0x13c`, `0x194da`/`0x19644`, and `compute_release_target` /
the per-tick service routine `0x13054` for whether ordinary note-off's
release target is quantised to a service tick, which would explain a
plateau-then-fall shape; this was not tested against the corpus and the
isolate's own resolution limits (low-register fundamental period 8-53 ms)
mean the 38 ms plateau length is not pinned precisely enough to fit a
two-segment model from the data gathered this session alone.

'''

[#item21-addendum]
== 2026-07-28 — Item 21 addendum 2026-07-27: the shape is SETTLED by `[M: probes 44/45]` -- it is a LINEAR ramp to zero, not a stretched exponential -- but implementing it alone REGRESSES the corpus, so the fitted floor stays

The original entry's "genuinely open" note guessed a delayed release onset
from one hand-cut isolate. Probes 44, 45 and 46 were built to settle it and
did, in a way that is both more specific and more awkward than the guess.

**What the floor actually is.** Probe 44 isolates one release per patch
against 900 ms of silence, 12 patches x 3 reps. Classified by level-crossing
regression -- a linear-to-zero ramp makes the crossing time linear in level,
a geometric one linear in log level -- validated in both directions before
use (it reads this project's own geometric render as geometric, R2 0.993 vs
0.837, and `tests/sine-gate.flac` as linear). Split by authored release, the
latter measured on this engine with the floor removed:

[options="header"]
|===
| authored `eg1_release_tc` | patches | reference shape
| 6.4 / 8.8 / 22.8 / 25.7 ms | square lead, sine 008:080, organ 1, nylon gt | **LINEAR to digital zero**
| 86 / 121 / 474-1972 ms | brass, flute, choir, piano, strings, pad, seashore | geometric
|===

For every geometric patch the reference's fall matches THIS ENGINE'S EXISTING
RATE to within 1-6% (ref / no-floor ratio 1.00, 1.00, 1.01, 1.01, 1.02, 1.06;
brass 1.30 and flute 1.17 sit nearest the crossover). The geometric segment
was never wrong. Only the floor was, and it was the wrong SHAPE, which is why
a duration had to be fitted to stand in for it.

**The ramp is a fixed duration, on the envelope.** Probe 45 sections B and C
sweep velocity 127..16 and CC7 127..16 on the sine, moving the level at
note-off over a 52x range (0.3087 -> 0.0059). The ramp measures 23.6 / 23.6 /
23.7 / 23.5 / 23.4 and 23.5 / 23.5 / 23.6 / 23.5 / 23.7 ms -- ten cases, sd
0.1 ms. A fixed RATE would have shrunk with the level by the same 52x. Being
unmoved by CC7 also puts the ramp on the envelope and not on the audible
level, the same distinction ``voice_step_envelope``'s finish detection makes.

**Still not established:** that 52x range is all GAIN. Every linear case
observed starts from a near-full EG, so "fixed duration" holds across gain
and not across envelope level. Probe 45's section A cannot settle it -- both
its patches (piano, marimba) have long authored releases and take the
geometric path. A note released mid-decay on a SHORT-release patch would.

**Why it is not shipped.** Implemented as `RELEASE_MIN_S = 0.0235` with a
linear branch in `ENV_RELEASE` (~10 lines), it improves its targeted probes
and fails the corpus gate:

[options="header"]
|===
| build | mean residual | mean env r | sine-gate | sine2 | p44 | p45
| HEAD: geometric, 60 ms floor | **-31.1483** | 0.9238 | -25.00 | -25.50 | -44.09 | -26.02
| linear 23.5 ms | -30.8095 | 0.9225 | -22.97 | -22.80 | **-44.96** | **-26.59**
| linear 23.5 ms + 6 ms note-off lead | -30.7313 | 0.9236 | -24.50 | -24.11 | -42.06 | -25.93
|===

63 items. Reverted; `RELEASE_FLOOR_S = 0.060` stays as the shipped
approximation.

**What is missing is the PLACEMENT, and that is now the live question.** The
original entry's "delayed onset" was the right instinct with the sign
inverted: the reference's ramp starts BEFORE the note-off. Probe 46 sweeps
the note-off across a 1.042 ms grid, 64 cases, half of them carrying a key-96
marker struck on the same tick so the earliness reads inside one file with no
reference alignment in the path. Both sections agree: the ramp starts 0-27 ms
ahead of the nominal note-off, mean 12.5 ms, spread 27.8 ms. Note-ONs in the
same captures are sample-exact, and two independent captures of
`tests/sine-gate.mid` reproduce the per-note pattern (r = 0.963, 32 of 34
notes agreeing to ~0.5 ms), so it is deterministic and not host scheduling.

The per-case step is startlingly quantised -- 15 of 31 steps are -9.8 ms with
sd 0.05 ms -- but the jumps between runs are +11.0 ms (x6) and +15.8 ms (x5),
two different sizes, so it is a lattice and not a fixed grid. A phase-free
first-harmonic scan over periods 4-60 ms finds nothing above the shuffled
null (0.407 vs 0.400). Consistent with ``[A:0x12bd6]``'s drain acting at the
start of the block containing the event, with a VARIABLE block size; not
consistent with any single block period.
